=== 1174 | run on into the next line | a group's own break / LIST_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            | None -> acc
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [], xs
    )

// `toInt` and `round` each work on one Decimal, so the Decimal comes last and the rounding policy
// leads: `d |> Decimal.round(2, HALF_UP)`. `divide` is a binary operation, so its two operands keep
// the order the mathematics writes them in and the policy follows them. The `(scale, mode)` pair
// therefore sits in front of the subject in one and behind the operands in the other, which is the
// two rules meeting rather than an accident.
let divide (dividend: Decimal, divisor: Decimal, scale: Int, mode: RoundingMode): Decimal
    | DivisionByZero = intrinsic "decimal.divide" let toInt (mode: RoundingMode, d: Decimal): Int = intrinsic "decimal.toInt"

let partition (keep: ('a) -> Bool, s: Set<'a>): (Set<'a>, Set<'a>) =
    Set.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (yes, no) = acc
            if keep(x) then (Set.insert(x, yes), no) else (yes, Set.insert(x, no))
        },

        (Set.empty, Set.empty),
        s
    )

=== 1717 | with a blank line under it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / APPLY_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc, k, v)

        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m)
    )

module souther.time

// The parts of a time, as `Date.year` / `month` / `day` are the parts of a date ([#stdlib-date]).
// All three are Ints: the hour 0 to 23, the minute and the second 0 to 59.
let hour (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.hour"
let minute (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.minute"
let second (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.second"

// There is no time arithmetic here. Adding to a time of day has to say what happens at midnight —
// whether 23:30 plus an hour is 00:30 or is nothing — and that is a decision about the domain
// rather than about the clock. A model holding the day as well adds on the `DateTime`, where the
// day is there to carry the answer; one holding only a time of day says what crossing midnight
// means as a rule of its own, since taking a `DateTime` to get the arithmetic would have it invent
// a date — which is what this type exists to avoid.

=== 757 | with a blank line under it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / LET_DESTRUCTURE / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(

        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

let updateIfPresent (key: 'k, f: ('a) -> 'a, m: Map<'k, 'a>): Map<'k, 'a> =
    match Map.get(key, m) with
        | None -> m
        | Some v -> Map.insert(key, f(v), m)


=== 1060 | with a blank line under it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match List.get(i, ys) with
                | Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                | None -> (next, pairs)

        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

        | A | B -> unreachable "neither reaches this rule"

=== 1183 | with a blank line under it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            | None -> acc

            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [],
        xs
    )

=== 723 | with a blank line under it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / a group's own break at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],

            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

// The first self-hosted standard-library function: logical negation, written in Souther rather

// An ordinary data: a mode is evaluated at the call like any other argument. The implementation is
// runtime-provided (souther.runtime.RoundingMode) rather than generated — see Prelude's
// runtime-backed registration. It has no codec: a rounding policy is a computation's input, not a
// value that crosses a serialization boundary.
data RoundingMode = HALF_UP | HALF_EVEN | HALF_DOWN | UP | DOWN | CEILING | FLOOR


=== 1485 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.option

// Consuming an `Option` (spec §algebraic-types) without leaving the value pipe. `List.get` / `Map.get`
// and `?` fields all yield an `Option`; before this module the only way to read one was a two-arm
// `match`, which dead-ends the pipe (spec §pipe). `withDefault` and `map` let the optional flow through
// `|>` — `Map.get(k, m) |> Option.map(v -> v.value) |> Option.withDefault("なし")`, as Elm's
// `Maybe.map`/`Maybe.withDefault` and F#'s `Option.map`/`Option.defaultValue` do.
//
// Argument order follows F#/Elm (spec §pipe): the option — the value being consumed — comes last, so
// it threads through the pipe. `Option` stays non-surface (spec §algebraic-types): the caller never
// names `Option<T>` nor builds a `Some`; the optional is inferred and unnamed as it flows here.
//
// `withDefault` only reads an option (a `match`, no construction), so it is an ordinary helper, expanded
// inline at its call site like `Map.updateOrInsert`. `map` must build a fresh `Some (f v)`, and `Option` is not
// surface-writable — a body cannot construct one — so `map` is a compiler built-in (like `List.get` /
// `List.find`), not a helper here; its signature is `map(f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>) : Option<'b>`.
// `andThen` is intentionally absent: its step would have to return an `Option`, which no user lambda can
// build under the non-surface rule (spec §algebraic-types).
let map (f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>): Option<'b> = intrinsic "option.map"


=== 304 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.bool

// The first self-hosted standard-library function: logical negation, written in Souther rather
// than as a primitive. Being a non-recursive helper, `not` is expanded inline at each call site,
// so no class is generated for it.
let not (b: Bool): Bool = if b then false else true


=== 757 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {

    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1058 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / a group's own break at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.set

// The higher-order operations (Elm `Set.map` / `filter` / `foldl` / `partition`). Like `Map`, `Set`
// adds no loop of its own: each one goes out through `toList`, uses the List combinator, and puts
// what it keeps back with `insert`, so `List.fold` stays the single primitive. The round trip is what a
// caller would otherwise write at every call site, and writing it there is what pushed set-shaped
// work into List land — `openIssue` in the issuetracker example cleaned its labels as a list purely
// because a set could not be mapped.
//
// `map` may shrink the set: two elements that land on the same image collapse, since a set holds no
// duplicates (Elm behaves the same way). `fold` runs over `toList`'s order, which is deterministic
// but unspecified, so a step whose result depends on the order is a mistake the compiler cannot
// catch — combine with something associative and commutative, as one would in Elm.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, s: Set<'a>): 'acc =

    List.fold(step, seed, Set.toList(s))

=== 815 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / MODULE_HEADER / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
// The Rational stdlib. Declared here like every other core module (§intrinsics); every kernel lives
// in souther-runtime's RationalMath, which owns the arithmetic of the exact quotient `/` answers
// (ADR-0112, ADR-0116).
//
// A Rational is a value a computation holds. It has no external form, so no field carries one, no
// behavior takes or answers one, and nothing here writes one at a boundary. What this module is for
// is the two directions across that edge: reaching exact arithmetic from a number a boundary did
// carry, and leaving it for one.
//
// The `Basics` arithmetic `Int` and `Decimal` carry — `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp` — is not here.
// Nothing asks for it yet, and each of them is an ordinary helper a model writes over the operators
// this module already has.
module souther.rational


=== 1429 | with a blank line under it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / a comment / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.instant
// A moment on the timeline (ADR-0028, [#stdlib-instant]). `Instant` is `java.time.Instant`, held to
// the nanosecond because that is what an outside timestamp says and dropping it would make a value
// written back out differ from the one that arrived.
//
// This module declares no function. An `Instant` has no year, no month and no hour until a zone says
// which day it fell on, and the language names no zone — the boundary owns it ([#primitives]). So
// there is nothing here to read a calendar part with, and nothing to build one from parts either:
// the two directions are absent together, which is what keeps this from being the hole
// [#stdlib-date] had. That the module is empty is the smaller fact — what the missing zone rules out
// is the calendar, and an operation staying on the timeline would need none. None is here because
// nothing has asked for one.
//
// What a model does with one it does without this module. Two `Instant`s compare, so a model says
// which came first with `<` ([#primitives]); one keys a `Map` ([#collections]); one is written as a
// literal in a fixture ([#temporal-literal]). Turning one into a `DateTime` needs the zone, so a
// model declares that as a behavior with no implementation and the Java side supplies it, exactly
// as it does for reading the current time ([#injected-behavior]):
//
//     behavior localDateTime : (at: Instant) -> DateTime

// than as a primitive. Being a non-recursive helper, `not` is expanded inline at each call site,

// so no class is generated for it.
let not (b: Bool): Bool = if b then false else true

=== 385 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.set

// `partition` answers the pair (kept, rest), the same shape `List.partition` gives.
let partition (keep: ('a) -> Bool, s: Set<'a>): (Set<'a>, Set<'a>) =
    Set.fold(

        (acc, x) -> {
            let (yes, no) = acc
            if keep(x) then (Set.insert(x, yes), no) else (yes, Set.insert(x, no))
        },
        (Set.empty, Set.empty),
        s
    )

=== 1717 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc, k, v)
        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m)

    )

=== 723 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),

            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 145 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / BINARY_EXPR / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

let allDistinctBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Bool =
    Set.size(Set.fromList(List.map(key, xs))) == List.length(xs)

module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same

=== 764 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / IF_EXPR / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 2 and column 4
// The same `Basics` arithmetic `Int` carries, on the same reasoning: `<` and `-` are defined for
// Decimal, so these are ordinary helpers. `abs` compares against `0.0m`, so a value whose scale
// differs still answers by numeric value, as `compare` does. `clamp(lo, hi, d)` keeps a rate or a
// ratio inside a range without a `match`, and the bounds keep their own scale — clamping returns one
// of the three values written, never a re-scaled copy.
let abs (d: Decimal): Decimal = if d < 0.0m then 0.0m - d else d
let min (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a < b then a else b
let max (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a > b then a else b
let clamp (lo: Decimal, hi: Decimal, d: Decimal): Decimal =
    if d < lo then lo else if d > hi then hi else d


=== 506 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / INTRINSIC_BODY / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
// Narrowing, exactly. What a carrier does not hold is a case and not a value chosen for it: three
// halves is no Int and a third is no Decimal, and neither answer is a rounding this was not told
// about. `4 / 2` and `1 / 2` go through these, requiring no rounding mode for a conversion that
// changes nothing.
let toWholeNumber (r: Rational): Int | NotWhole = intrinsic "rational.toWholeNumber"
let toFiniteDecimal (r: Rational): Decimal | NotAFiniteDecimal =
    intrinsic "rational.toFiniteDecimal"

=== 1183 | with a blank line under it | a group's own break / LIST_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            | None -> acc
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [],

        xs
    )

    match o with

        | Some(W(x)) -> x
        | Mod.None { a, b = c } as whole -> 0
        | A | B -> unreachable "neither reaches this rule"

=== 1717 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / APPLY_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc, k, v) 
        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m)
    )

=== 890 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / FN_DEF / NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.time

// The parts of a time, as `Date.year` / `month` / `day` are the parts of a date ([#stdlib-date]).
// All three are Ints: the hour 0 to 23, the minute and the second 0 to 59.
let hour (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.hour"
let minute (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.minute"
let second (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.second" 
// There is no time arithmetic here. Adding to a time of day has to say what happens at midnight —
// whether 23:30 plus an hour is 00:30 or is nothing — and that is a decision about the domain
// rather than about the clock. A model holding the day as well adds on the `DateTime`, where the
// day is there to carry the answer; one holding only a time of day says what crossing midnight
// means as a rule of its own, since taking a `DateTime` to get the arithmetic would have it invent
// a date — which is what this type exists to avoid.

=== 757 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / LET_DESTRUCTURE / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold( 
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 193 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 3 and column 8

let updateIfPresent (key: 'k, f: ('a) -> 'a, m: Map<'k, 'a>): Map<'k, 'a> =
    match Map.get(key, m) with
        | None -> m
        | Some v -> Map.insert(key, f(v), m) 

=== 1060 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match List.get(i, ys) with
                | Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                | None -> (next, pairs) 
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1183 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            | None -> acc 
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [],
        xs
    )

=== 723 | with a space at the end of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / a group's own break at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x], 
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 1485 | with a space at the end of it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.option

// Consuming an `Option` (spec §algebraic-types) without leaving the value pipe. `List.get` / `Map.get`
// and `?` fields all yield an `Option`; before this module the only way to read one was a two-arm
// `match`, which dead-ends the pipe (spec §pipe). `withDefault` and `map` let the optional flow through
// `|>` — `Map.get(k, m) |> Option.map(v -> v.value) |> Option.withDefault("なし")`, as Elm's
// `Maybe.map`/`Maybe.withDefault` and F#'s `Option.map`/`Option.defaultValue` do.
//
// Argument order follows F#/Elm (spec §pipe): the option — the value being consumed — comes last, so
// it threads through the pipe. `Option` stays non-surface (spec §algebraic-types): the caller never
// names `Option<T>` nor builds a `Some`; the optional is inferred and unnamed as it flows here.
//
// `withDefault` only reads an option (a `match`, no construction), so it is an ordinary helper, expanded
// inline at its call site like `Map.updateOrInsert`. `map` must build a fresh `Some (f v)`, and `Option` is not
// surface-writable — a body cannot construct one — so `map` is a compiler built-in (like `List.get` /
// `List.find`), not a helper here; its signature is `map(f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>) : Option<'b>`.
// `andThen` is intentionally absent: its step would have to return an `Option`, which no user lambda can
// build under the non-surface rule (spec §algebraic-types).
let map (f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>): Option<'b> = intrinsic "option.map"

=== 849 | with a space at the end of it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.datetime

// DateTime arithmetic (ADR-0028, [#stdlib-datetime]). `DateTime` is `java.time.LocalDateTime`, a
// local date-time with no zone — the boundary owns the zone ([#primitives]). Each `let` names a JVM
// operation the backend emits. Like Date arithmetic these are pure, so they belong in the domain.
//
// Argument order is F#/Elm ([#pipe]): the date-time operated on comes last. `minutesBetween(from,
// to)` counts whole minutes; `toDate` drops the time of day, yielding a `Date`.
let addMinutes (minutes: Int, dt: DateTime): DateTime = intrinsic "datetime.addMinutes" 
let addHours (hours: Int, dt: DateTime): DateTime = intrinsic "datetime.addHours"
let addDays (days: Int, dt: DateTime): DateTime = intrinsic "datetime.addDays"
let minutesBetween (from: DateTime, to: DateTime): Int = intrinsic "datetime.minutesBetween"

=== 1058 | with a space at the end of it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / a group's own break at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.set

// The higher-order operations (Elm `Set.map` / `filter` / `foldl` / `partition`). Like `Map`, `Set`
// adds no loop of its own: each one goes out through `toList`, uses the List combinator, and puts
// what it keeps back with `insert`, so `List.fold` stays the single primitive. The round trip is what a
// caller would otherwise write at every call site, and writing it there is what pushed set-shaped
// work into List land — `openIssue` in the issuetracker example cleaned its labels as a list purely
// because a set could not be mapped.
//
// `map` may shrink the set: two elements that land on the same image collapse, since a set holds no
// duplicates (Elm behaves the same way). `fold` runs over `toList`'s order, which is deterministic
// but unspecified, so a step whose result depends on the order is a mistake the compiler cannot
// catch — combine with something associative and commutative, as one would in Elm.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, s: Set<'a>): 'acc = 
    List.fold(step, seed, Set.toList(s))

=== 815 | with a space at the end of it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / MODULE_HEADER / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
// The Rational stdlib. Declared here like every other core module (§intrinsics); every kernel lives
// in souther-runtime's RationalMath, which owns the arithmetic of the exact quotient `/` answers
// (ADR-0112, ADR-0116).
//
// A Rational is a value a computation holds. It has no external form, so no field carries one, no
// behavior takes or answers one, and nothing here writes one at a boundary. What this module is for
// is the two directions across that edge: reaching exact arithmetic from a number a boundary did
// carry, and leaving it for one.
//
// The `Basics` arithmetic `Int` and `Decimal` carry — `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp` — is not here.
// Nothing asks for it yet, and each of them is an ordinary helper a model writes over the operators
// this module already has.
module souther.rational 

=== 1429 | with a space at the end of it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / a comment / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.instant
// A moment on the timeline (ADR-0028, [#stdlib-instant]). `Instant` is `java.time.Instant`, held to
// the nanosecond because that is what an outside timestamp says and dropping it would make a value
// written back out differ from the one that arrived.
//
// This module declares no function. An `Instant` has no year, no month and no hour until a zone says
// which day it fell on, and the language names no zone — the boundary owns it ([#primitives]). So
// there is nothing here to read a calendar part with, and nothing to build one from parts either:
// the two directions are absent together, which is what keeps this from being the hole
// [#stdlib-date] had. That the module is empty is the smaller fact — what the missing zone rules out
// is the calendar, and an operation staying on the timeline would need none. None is here because
// nothing has asked for one.
//
// What a model does with one it does without this module. Two `Instant`s compare, so a model says
// which came first with `<` ([#primitives]); one keys a `Map` ([#collections]); one is written as a
// literal in a fixture ([#temporal-literal]). Turning one into a `DateTime` needs the zone, so a
// model declares that as a behavior with no implementation and the Java side supplies it, exactly
// as it does for reading the current time ([#injected-behavior]):
//
//     behavior localDateTime : (at: Instant) -> DateTime

// The higher-order operations (Elm `Set.map` / `filter` / `foldl` / `partition`). Like `Map`, `Set`

=== 433 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

let map (f: ('a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc ++ [f(x)], [], xs)
let filter (keep: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): List<'a> =
    List.fold((acc, x) -> if keep(x) then acc ++ [x] else acc, [], xs) 
let all (p: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): Bool = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc && p(x), true, xs)
let any (p: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): Bool = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc || p(x), false, xs)

=== 385 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.set

// `partition` answers the pair (kept, rest), the same shape `List.partition` gives.
let partition (keep: ('a) -> Bool, s: Set<'a>): (Set<'a>, Set<'a>) =
    Set.fold( 
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (yes, no) = acc
            if keep(x) then (Set.insert(x, yes), no) else (yes, Set.insert(x, no))
        },
        (Set.empty, Set.empty),
        s
    )

=== 1717 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc, k, v)
        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m) 
    )

=== 723 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x), 
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 145 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / BINARY_EXPR / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

let allDistinctBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Bool =
    Set.size(Set.fromList(List.map(key, xs))) == List.length(xs)

=== 764 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / IF_EXPR / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 2 and column 4
// The same `Basics` arithmetic `Int` carries, on the same reasoning: `<` and `-` are defined for
// Decimal, so these are ordinary helpers. `abs` compares against `0.0m`, so a value whose scale
// differs still answers by numeric value, as `compare` does. `clamp(lo, hi, d)` keeps a rate or a
// ratio inside a range without a `match`, and the bounds keep their own scale — clamping returns one
// of the three values written, never a re-scaled copy.
let abs (d: Decimal): Decimal = if d < 0.0m then 0.0m - d else d
let min (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a < b then a else b
let max (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a > b then a else b
let clamp (lo: Decimal, hi: Decimal, d: Decimal): Decimal =
    if d < lo then lo else if d > hi then hi else d 

=== 506 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / INTRINSIC_BODY / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
// Narrowing, exactly. What a carrier does not hold is a case and not a value chosen for it: three
// halves is no Int and a third is no Decimal, and neither answer is a rounding this was not told
// about. `4 / 2` and `1 / 2` go through these, requiring no rounding mode for a conversion that
// changes nothing.
let toWholeNumber (r: Rational): Int | NotWhole = intrinsic "rational.toWholeNumber"
let toFiniteDecimal (r: Rational): Decimal | NotAFiniteDecimal =
    intrinsic "rational.toFiniteDecimal" 

=== 696 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / INTRINSIC_BODY / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
// `truncatingRemainder` is the remainder of a division whose quotient is truncated toward zero, so
// its result takes the sign of the dividend: `truncatingRemainder(-7, 3)` is `-1`. The name carries
// that, because a bare `remainder` (or `mod`) means the truncated one in some languages and the
// floored one in others, and the two disagree on exactly the values a domain is least likely to test.
let truncatingRemainder (dividend: Int, divisor: Int): Int | DivisionByZero =
    intrinsic "int.truncatingRemainder" 
let add (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.add"
let subtract (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.subtract"
let multiply (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.multiply"

=== 1183 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / LIST_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            | None -> acc
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [], 
        xs
    )

let f (o: Option<W>): Int =
    match o with 
        | Some(W(x)) -> x
        | Mod.None { a, b = c } as whole -> 0
        | A | B -> unreachable "neither reaches this rule"

=== 753 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / no place / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
// `toInt` and `round` each work on one Decimal, so the Decimal comes last and the rounding policy
// leads: `d |> Decimal.round(2, HALF_UP)`. `divide` is a binary operation, so its two operands keep
// the order the mathematics writes them in and the policy follows them. The `(scale, mode)` pair
// therefore sits in front of the subject in one and behind the operands in the other, which is the
// two rules meeting rather than an accident.
let divide (dividend: Decimal, divisor: Decimal, scale: Int, mode: RoundingMode): Decimal
    | DivisionByZero = intrinsic "decimal.divide" 
let toInt (mode: RoundingMode, d: Decimal): Int = intrinsic "decimal.toInt"
let round (scale: Int, mode: RoundingMode, d: Decimal): Decimal = intrinsic "decimal.round"

=== 723 | with a space at the end of it | a group's own break / no place / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ), 
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 1429 | with a space at the end of it | the start of the file / SOURCE_FILE / NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE at depth 0 and column 0
module souther.instant 
// A moment on the timeline (ADR-0028, [#stdlib-instant]). `Instant` is `java.time.Instant`, held to
// the nanosecond because that is what an outside timestamp says and dropping it would make a value
// written back out differ from the one that arrived.
//
// This module declares no function. An `Instant` has no year, no month and no hour until a zone says
// which day it fell on, and the language names no zone — the boundary owns it ([#primitives]). So
// there is nothing here to read a calendar part with, and nothing to build one from parts either:
// the two directions are absent together, which is what keeps this from being the hole
// [#stdlib-date] had. That the module is empty is the smaller fact — what the missing zone rules out
// is the calendar, and an operation staying on the timeline would need none. None is here because
// nothing has asked for one.
//
// What a model does with one it does without this module. Two `Instant`s compare, so a model says
// which came first with `<` ([#primitives]); one keys a `Map` ([#collections]); one is written as a
// literal in a fixture ([#temporal-literal]). Turning one into a `DateTime` needs the zone, so a
// model declares that as a behavior with no implementation and the Java side supplies it, exactly
// as it does for reading the current time ([#injected-behavior]):
//
//     behavior localDateTime : (at: Instant) -> DateTime

=== 815 | with a space at the end of it | the start of the file / a comment / NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE at depth 0 and column 0
// The Rational stdlib. Declared here like every other core module (§intrinsics); every kernel lives 
// in souther-runtime's RationalMath, which owns the arithmetic of the exact quotient `/` answers
// (ADR-0112, ADR-0116).
//
// A Rational is a value a computation holds. It has no external form, so no field carries one, no
// behavior takes or answers one, and nothing here writes one at a boundary. What this module is for
// is the two directions across that edge: reaching exact arithmetic from a number a boundary did
// carry, and leaving it for one.
//
// The `Basics` arithmetic `Int` and `Decimal` carry — `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp` — is not here.
// Nothing asks for it yet, and each of them is an ordinary helper a model writes over the operators
// this module already has.

=== 1717 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / APPLY_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc,  k, v)
        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m)
    )

module souther.time

// The parts of a time, as `Date.year` / `month` / `day` are the parts of a date ([#stdlib-date]).
// All three are Ints: the hour 0 to 23, the minute and the second 0 to 59.
let hour (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.hour"
let minute (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.minute"
let  second (t: Time): Int = intrinsic "time.second"
// There is no time arithmetic here. Adding to a time of day has to say what happens at midnight —
// whether 23:30 plus an hour is 00:30 or is nothing — and that is a decision about the domain
// rather than about the clock. A model holding the day as well adds on the `DateTime`, where the
// day is there to carry the answer; one holding only a time of day says what crossing midnight
// means as a rule of its own, since taking a `DateTime` to get the arithmetic would have it invent
// a date — which is what this type exists to avoid.

=== 757 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / LET_DESTRUCTURE / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let  (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1060 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match List.get(i, ys) with
                | Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                |  None -> (next, pairs)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

        | A | B -> unreachable "neither reaches this rule"

=== 1183 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            |  None -> acc
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [],
        xs
    )

=== 723 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / a group's own break at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                |  Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 1060 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_EXPR / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match  List.get(i, ys) with
                | Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                | None -> (next, pairs)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 757 | with a space doubled in it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / TUPLE_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i  + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1485 | with a space doubled in it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.option

// Consuming an `Option` (spec §algebraic-types) without leaving the value pipe. `List.get` / `Map.get`
// and `?` fields all yield an `Option`; before this module the only way to read one was a two-arm
// `match`, which dead-ends the pipe (spec §pipe). `withDefault` and `map` let the optional flow through
// `|>` — `Map.get(k, m) |> Option.map(v -> v.value) |> Option.withDefault("なし")`, as Elm's
// `Maybe.map`/`Maybe.withDefault` and F#'s `Option.map`/`Option.defaultValue` do.
//
// Argument order follows F#/Elm (spec §pipe): the option — the value being consumed — comes last, so
// it threads through the pipe. `Option` stays non-surface (spec §algebraic-types): the caller never
// names `Option<T>` nor builds a `Some`; the optional is inferred and unnamed as it flows here.
//
// `withDefault` only reads an option (a `match`, no construction), so it is an ordinary helper, expanded
// inline at its call site like `Map.updateOrInsert`. `map` must build a fresh `Some (f v)`, and `Option` is not
// surface-writable — a body cannot construct one — so `map` is a compiler built-in (like `List.get` /
// `List.find`), not a helper here; its signature is `map(f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>) : Option<'b>`.
// `andThen` is intentionally absent: its step would have to return an `Option`, which no user lambda can
// build under the non-surface rule (spec §algebraic-types).
let  map (f: ('a) -> 'b, opt: Option<'a>): Option<'b> = intrinsic "option.map"

// so no class is generated for it.
let  not (b: Bool): Bool = if b then false else true

=== 1058 | with a space doubled in it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / FN_DEF / a group's own break at depth 1 and column 0
module souther.set

// The higher-order operations (Elm `Set.map` / `filter` / `foldl` / `partition`). Like `Map`, `Set`
// adds no loop of its own: each one goes out through `toList`, uses the List combinator, and puts
// what it keeps back with `insert`, so `List.fold` stays the single primitive. The round trip is what a
// caller would otherwise write at every call site, and writing it there is what pushed set-shaped
// work into List land — `openIssue` in the issuetracker example cleaned its labels as a list purely
// because a set could not be mapped.
//
// `map` may shrink the set: two elements that land on the same image collapse, since a set holds no
// duplicates (Elm behaves the same way). `fold` runs over `toList`'s order, which is deterministic
// but unspecified, so a step whose result depends on the order is a mistake the compiler cannot
// catch — combine with something associative and commutative, as one would in Elm.
let  fold (step: ('acc, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, s: Set<'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(step, seed, Set.toList(s))

=== 815 | with a space doubled in it | NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE / MODULE_HEADER / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 1 and column 0
// The Rational stdlib. Declared here like every other core module (§intrinsics); every kernel lives
// in souther-runtime's RationalMath, which owns the arithmetic of the exact quotient `/` answers
// (ADR-0112, ADR-0116).
//
// A Rational is a value a computation holds. It has no external form, so no field carries one, no
// behavior takes or answers one, and nothing here writes one at a boundary. What this module is for
// is the two directions across that edge: reaching exact arithmetic from a number a boundary did
// carry, and leaving it for one.

// The `Basics` arithmetic `Int` and `Decimal` carry — `abs`, `min`, `max`, `clamp` — is not here.
// Nothing asks for it yet, and each of them is an ordinary helper a model writes over the operators
// this module already has.
module  souther.rational

=== 764 | with a space doubled in it | a group's own break / IF_EXPR / A_FILE_ENDS_WITH_ONE_NEWLINE at depth 2 and column 4
// The same `Basics` arithmetic `Int` carries, on the same reasoning: `<` and `-` are defined for
// Decimal, so these are ordinary helpers. `abs` compares against `0.0m`, so a value whose scale
// differs still answers by numeric value, as `compare` does. `clamp(lo, hi, d)` keeps a rate or a
// ratio inside a range without a `match`, and the bounds keep their own scale — clamping returns one
// of the three values written, never a re-scaled copy.
let abs (d: Decimal): Decimal = if d < 0.0m then 0.0m - d else d
let min (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a < b then a else b
let max (a: Decimal, b: Decimal): Decimal = if a > b then a else b
let clamp (lo: Decimal, hi: Decimal, d: Decimal): Decimal =
    if  d < lo then lo else if d > hi then hi else d

// `truncatingRemainder` is the remainder of a division whose quotient is truncated toward zero, so

=== 723 | with a space doubled in it | a group's own break / LAMBDA_EXPR / a group's own break at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc,  x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                | Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

// `toInt` and `round` each work on one Decimal, so the Decimal comes last and the rounding policy
// leads: `d |> Decimal.round(2, HALF_UP)`. `divide` is a binary operation, so its two operands keep

=== 1429 | with a space doubled in it | the start of the file / SOURCE_FILE / NOTHING_SHARES_A_COMMENTS_LINE at depth 0 and column 0
module  souther.instant
// A moment on the timeline (ADR-0028, [#stdlib-instant]). `Instant` is `java.time.Instant`, held to
// the nanosecond because that is what an outside timestamp says and dropping it would make a value
// written back out differ from the one that arrived.
//
// This module declares no function. An `Instant` has no year, no month and no hour until a zone says
// which day it fell on, and the language names no zone — the boundary owns it ([#primitives]). So
// there is nothing here to read a calendar part with, and nothing to build one from parts either:
// the two directions are absent together, which is what keeps this from being the hole
// [#stdlib-date] had. That the module is empty is the smaller fact — what the missing zone rules out
// is the calendar, and an operation staying on the timeline would need none. None is here because
// nothing has asked for one.
//
// What a model does with one it does without this module. Two `Instant`s compare, so a model says
// which came first with `<` ([#primitives]); one keys a `Map` ([#collections]); one is written as a
// literal in a fixture ([#temporal-literal]). Turning one into a `DateTime` needs the zone, so a
// model declares that as a behavior with no implementation and the Java side supplies it, exactly
// as it does for reading the current time ([#injected-behavior]):
//
//     behavior localDateTime : (at: Instant) -> DateTime

=== 1715 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / APPLY_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.map

// The higher-order operations, the only ones taking a step function. `Map` adds no privileged loop:
// `fold` runs `List.fold` over the entries (`toList`, [#stdlib-map]), so the one list-fold loop
// stays the sole primitive. The entries fold in the map's deterministic hash order (`toList`'s
// order), so a fold that combines its values reproduces the same result each run.
//
// `fold`'s step is `(acc, key, value)` (F#'s `Map.fold` folder is `state key value`, Elm's
// `Dict.foldl` is `k v acc`); the entry tuple is destructured and passed on. It is `fold`, not
// `foldLeft`, for the reason `Set.fold` is: a map publishes no first and no last entry, so there is
// no left for a right to be the counterpart of. `List` is where that distinction exists.
//
// `mapValues` keeps the keys and rewrites the values, growing a fresh map through `fold`. The name
// says which half moves — a bare `map` on a map leaves a reader to guess whether the keys, the
// values or the entries are what `f` answers for. The step still sees the key, so a value may be
// rewritten in terms of the key it is filed under.
//
// `updateIfPresent` rewrites the value under one key with `f`; an absent key is a no-op, which is
// what the name says and what `update` alone would not. Its step is `('a) -> 'a`, not Elm's
// `Maybe v -> Maybe v`: `Option` is not surface-writable ([#algebraic-types]), so a step cannot
// return one — insert a new key with `insert` and drop one with `remove`.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'k, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, m: Map<'k, 'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(
        (acc, e) -> {
            let (k, v) = e
            step(acc,k, v)
        },
        seed,
        Map.toList(m)
    )

=== 755 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / LET_DESTRUCTURE / a group's own break at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let(_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i + 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 191 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 3 and column 8

let updateIfPresent (key: 'k, f: ('a) -> 'a, m: Map<'k, 'a>): Map<'k, 'a> =
    match Map.get(key, m) with
        | None -> m
        |Some v -> Map.insert(key, f(v), m)

=== 1058 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match List.get(i, ys) with
                | Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                |None -> (next, pairs)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1181 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `filterMap` maps and drops in one pass (Elm's `List.filterMap`, F#'s `List.choose`): `f` answers
// with an optional, and only what is there joins the result. This is how an optional field becomes a
// plain list — `filterMap(i -> i.assignee, issues)` — without the caller writing a sentinel value to
// filter on afterwards. `f`'s result type is written `'b?`, an optional in a signature, which is a
// core privilege like the type variables around it: the caller's step returns an optional it read
// (a `?` field, `Map.get`, `List.find`) and never names the type (ADR-0011). The step can be named
// as well as written inline — a helper that leaves its result type off has the optional inferred.
//
// So `filterMap` serves the absence a model reads. Absence the model owns — a sum case with nothing
// to give — cannot reach it, because nothing but the core builds an optional; that projection answers
// a list of nought or one and `flatMap` joins them.
let filterMap (f: ('a) -> 'b?, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> match f(x) with
            |None -> acc
            | Some v -> acc ++ [v],
        [],
        xs
    )

=== 1058 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `zipShortest` pairs two lists element by element, and `unzip` takes the pairs apart again. The
// pair a caller wants out of two parallel lists is a tuple, which Souther has, so these two cover
// what Elm reaches `map2` for: `zipShortest(xs, ys) |> map(f)` is `map2(f, xs, ys)`.
//
// The name says what a length mismatch does, because languages disagree: Elm's `map2` truncates,
// F#'s `List.zip` raises. This one truncates to the shorter list, and says so rather than leaving
// the caller to find out. It walks `xs` with the same `(i, ys)` pair accumulator `mapIndexed` uses
// and reads the partner with `List.get`, so a missing partner simply ends the pairing.
let zipShortest (xs: List<'a>, ys: List<'b>): List<('a, 'b)> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, pairs) = acc
            let next = i + 1
            match List.get(i, ys) with
                |Some y -> (next, pairs ++ [(x, y)])
                | None -> (next, pairs)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 721 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / MATCH_CASE / a group's own break at depth 5 and column 16
module souther.list

// `groupBy` buckets the elements by a key into a `Map<'k, List<'a>>`, each bucket in first-seen
// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same
// way `distinct` grows a list, its key and value types recovered from the block.
let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =
    List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> Map.insert(
            key(x),
            match Map.get(key(x), acc) with
                | None -> [x]
                |Some bucket -> bucket ++ [x],
            acc
        ),
        Map.empty,
        xs
    )

=== 755 | with a space taken out of it | MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN / TUPLE_EXPR / A_BRACKET_TAKES_A_LINE_OF_ITS_OWN at depth 4 and column 12
module souther.list

// `mapIndexed` applies `f(index, element)` down the list, the index starting at 0. `fold` carries no
// index of its own, so it threads one in a pair accumulator `(i, ys)` — the same tuple-accumulator
// shape as `distinct`/`partition` below — and drops the counter at the end. It saves the caller from
// hand-rolling that `(i, acc)` fold for a weighted sum or a positional transform (a check-digit
// weighting reads `mapIndexed((i, c) -> w(i) * d(c)) |> sum`).
let mapIndexed (f: (Int, 'a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = {
    let (_, out) = List.fold(
        (acc, x) -> {
            let (i, ys) = acc
            let mapped = ys ++ [f(i, x)]
            (i+ 1, mapped)
        },
        (0, []),
        xs
    )
    out
}

=== 1056 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.set

// The higher-order operations (Elm `Set.map` / `filter` / `foldl` / `partition`). Like `Map`, `Set`
// adds no loop of its own: each one goes out through `toList`, uses the List combinator, and puts
// what it keeps back with `insert`, so `List.fold` stays the single primitive. The round trip is what a
// caller would otherwise write at every call site, and writing it there is what pushed set-shaped
// work into List land — `openIssue` in the issuetracker example cleaned its labels as a list purely
// because a set could not be mapped.
//
// `map` may shrink the set: two elements that land on the same image collapse, since a set holds no
// duplicates (Elm behaves the same way). `fold` runs over `toList`'s order, which is deterministic
// but unspecified, so a step whose result depends on the order is a mistake the compiler cannot
// catch — combine with something associative and commutative, as one would in Elm.
let fold (step: ('acc, 'a) -> 'acc, seed: 'acc, s: Set<'a>): 'acc =
    List.fold(step,seed, Set.toList(s))

=== 431 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / APPLY_EXPR / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
module souther.list

let map (f: ('a) -> 'b, xs: List<'a>): List<'b> = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc ++ [f(x)], [], xs)
let filter (keep: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): List<'a> =
    List.fold((acc,x) -> if keep(x) then acc ++ [x] else acc, [], xs)
let all (p: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): Bool = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc && p(x), true, xs)
let any (p: ('a) -> Bool, xs: List<'a>): Bool = List.fold((acc, x) -> acc || p(x), false, xs)

module souther.list

let allDistinctBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Bool =
    Set.size(Set.fromList(List.map(key,xs))) == List.length(xs)

=== 504 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / INTRINSIC_BODY / A_BLANK_LINE_SEPARATES_TOP_LEVEL_ITEMS at depth 2 and column 4
// Narrowing, exactly. What a carrier does not hold is a case and not a value chosen for it: three
// halves is no Int and a third is no Decimal, and neither answer is a rounding this was not told
// about. `4 / 2` and `1 / 2` go through these, requiring no rounding mode for a conversion that
// changes nothing.
let toWholeNumber (r: Rational): Int | NotWhole = intrinsic "rational.toWholeNumber"
let toFiniteDecimal (r: Rational): Decimal | NotAFiniteDecimal =

=== 694 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / INTRINSIC_BODY / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
// `truncatingRemainder` is the remainder of a division whose quotient is truncated toward zero, so
// its result takes the sign of the dividend: `truncatingRemainder(-7, 3)` is `-1`. The name carries
// that, because a bare `remainder` (or `mod`) means the truncated one in some languages and the
// floored one in others, and the two disagree on exactly the values a domain is least likely to test.
let truncatingRemainder (dividend: Int, divisor: Int): Int | DivisionByZero =
    intrinsic"int.truncatingRemainder"
let add (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.add"
let subtract (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.subtract"
let multiply (a: Int, b: Int): Int = intrinsic "int.multiply"

=== 383 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / LAMBDA_EXPR / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 3 and column 8
module souther.set

// `partition` answers the pair (kept, rest), the same shape `List.partition` gives.
let partition (keep: ('a) -> Bool, s: Set<'a>): (Set<'a>, Set<'a>) =
    Set.fold(
        (acc,x) -> {
            let (yes, no) = acc
            if keep(x) then (Set.insert(x, yes), no) else (yes, Set.insert(x, no))
        },
        (Set.empty, Set.empty),
        s
    )

// order (Elm's Dict-based grouping). It folds `Map.empty`, reading the current bucket with `Map.get`
// (a `None` starts a new one) and writing back the extended bucket — a Map accumulator grown the same

let groupBy (key: ('a) -> 'k, xs: List<'a>): Map<'k, List<'a>> =

=== 751 | with a space taken out of it | a group's own break / no place / MEMBERS_TAKE_LINES_OF_THEIR_OWN at depth 2 and column 4
// `toInt` and `round` each work on one Decimal, so the Decimal comes last and the rounding policy
// leads: `d |> Decimal.round(2, HALF_UP)`. `divide` is a binary operation, so its two operands keep
// the order the mathematics writes them in and the policy follows them. The `(scale, mode)` pair
// therefore sits in front of the subject in one and behind the operands in the other, which is the
// two rules meeting rather than an accident.
let divide (dividend: Decimal, divisor: Decimal, scale: Int, mode: RoundingMode): Decimal
    |DivisionByZero = intrinsic "decimal.divide"
let toInt (mode: RoundingMode, d: Decimal): Int = intrinsic "decimal.toInt"
let round (scale: Int, mode: RoundingMode, d: Decimal): Decimal = intrinsic "decimal.round"
